I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, not in the same way. I mean, I can still do things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just doing nothing all the time. But the things I used to actually look forward to—dance class, hiking, trying new recipes, game night—they mostly just feel like chores now. I keep putting them off, even though they used to make me feel like myself. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I haven’t been checking closely or anything. My clothes seem to fit about the same, so I don’t think there’s been a big change. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it a problem exactly. I mean, my sleep probably isn’t perfect, but I haven’t really been tracking it. Some nights are better than others, I guess. Is there a specific kind of sleep problem you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I’m not sure it’s more than normal. I mean, I still get through the day. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Some days I feel kind of drained, I guess, but I don’t know if that counts as “more tired than usual.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about things?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly. But most days there’s kind of this… heavy, sour feeling underneath everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even when nothing is actually happening. I know that probably sounds strange. I try not to dwell on it, but it’s there a lot.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I get scared sometimes, and my mind goes to dark places, but I’m not sitting there thinking I want to die or anything like that. I don’t want you to think it’s like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really noticed. I mean, I might be a little tense sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m moving really slow or speeding around or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve noticed I just don’t really care about things the way I used to. It’s not like I’m trying to be difficult or anything, but dance class, hiking, trying new recipes, even game night… they all kind of feel like chores now, and I keep putting them off. Those things used to make me feel like myself, so… I thought maybe I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly… I mean, not all the time. It’s more like most days there’s this low, sour feeling underneath everything, if that makes sense. Like fear or guilt, even when nothing bad is actually happening. It’s not dramatic, I can still get through the day, but it’s kind of… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things when you're actually doing them, even if you don't feel like starting them?\n\nPatient: Sometimes a little, I guess… but not like before. Even if I make myself go to dance or show up for game night, it kind of still feels like I’m just getting through it, not really enjoying it. Hiking, cooking, all those things used to feel like me, and now they just sit there like tasks I keep avoiding. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, not in the same way. I mean, I can still do things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just doing nothing all the time. But the things I used to actually look forward to—dance class, hiking, trying new recipes, game night—they mostly just feel like chores now. I keep putting them off, even though they used to make me feel like myself. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I haven’t been checking closely or anything. My clothes seem to fit about the same, so I don’t think there’s been a big change. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it a problem exactly. I mean, my sleep probably isn’t perfect, but I haven’t really been tracking it. Some nights are better than others, I guess. Is there a specific kind of sleep problem you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I’m not sure it’s more than normal. I mean, I still get through the day. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Some days I feel kind of drained, I guess, but I don’t know if that counts as “more tired than usual.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about things?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly. But most days there’s kind of this… heavy, sour feeling underneath everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even when nothing is actually happening. I know that probably sounds strange. I try not to dwell on it, but it’s there a lot.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I get scared sometimes, and my mind goes to dark places, but I’m not sitting there thinking I want to die or anything like that. I don’t want you to think it’s like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really noticed. I mean, I might be a little tense sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m moving really slow or speeding around or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or on edge?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. Like in parking lots, or when I’m picking someone up, I’m always kind of checking who’s around me, where my car is, how quickly I could get out if I needed to. Everyone else seems calm, so I know it might sound like too much… but I just feel better if I’ve noticed those things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been since that robbery at the festival job. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve noticed I just don’t really care about things the way I used to. I mean, my sleep probably isn’t perfect, but I haven’t really been tracking it. Some nights are better than others, I guess. I mean, I might be a little tense sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m moving really slow or speeding around or anything. It’s not like I’m trying to be difficult or anything, but dance class, hiking, trying new recipes, even game night… they all kind of feel like chores now, and I keep putting them off. Those things used to make me feel like myself, so… I thought maybe I should talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly… I mean, not all the time. It’s more like most days there’s this low, sour feeling underneath everything, if that makes sense. Like fear or guilt, even when nothing bad is actually happening. It’s not dramatic, I can still get through the day, but it’s kind of… there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things when you're actually doing them, even if you don't feel like starting them?\n\nPatient: Sometimes a little, I guess… but not like before. Even if I make myself go to dance or show up for game night, it kind of still feels like I’m just getting through it, not really enjoying it. Hiking, cooking, all those things used to feel like me, and now they just sit there like tasks I keep avoiding. I don’t know if that’s the right way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, not in the same way. I mean, I can still do things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just doing nothing all the time. But the things I used to actually look forward to—dance class, hiking, trying new recipes, game night—they mostly just feel like chores now. I keep putting them off, even though they used to make me feel like myself. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I haven’t been checking closely or anything. My clothes seem to fit about the same, so I don’t think there’s been a big change. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it a problem exactly. I mean, my sleep probably isn’t perfect, but I haven’t really been tracking it. Some nights are better than others, I guess. Is there a specific kind of sleep problem you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I’m not sure it’s more than normal. I mean, I still get through the day. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Some days I feel kind of drained, I guess, but I don’t know if that counts as “more tired than usual.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about things?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I feel worthless exactly. But most days there’s kind of this… heavy, sour feeling underneath everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even when nothing is actually happening. I know that probably sounds strange. I try not to dwell on it, but it’s there a lot.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I get scared sometimes, and my mind goes to dark places, but I’m not sitting there thinking I want to die or anything like that. I don’t want you to think it’s like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really noticed. I mean, I might be a little tense sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’m moving really slow or speeding around or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or on edge?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. Like in parking lots, or when I’m picking someone up, I’m always kind of checking who’s around me, where my car is, how quickly I could get out if I needed to. Everyone else seems calm, so I know it might sound like too much… but I just feel better if I’ve noticed those things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been since that robbery at the festival job. So, about two years and three months now. I didn’t really think of it as a “thing” at first, but… yeah, that’s when it started.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this low, sour feeling you've described is present every day?\n\nPatient: Most days, yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I can still get through things. But there’s usually this kind of low, sour feeling under everything… like fear, and some guilt too, even when nothing bad is actually happening. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say this feeling of not caring about things and the low, sour feeling are interfering with your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still managing, so I don’t want it to sound like I’ve just fallen apart. But I’ve missed some Chamber application deadlines, and I keep putting my catering plans on hold because the paperwork and dealing with jobs and people just feels harder than it used to.\n\nAnd socially… I see friends less. Not because I don’t care about them, exactly, but my life has kind of gotten smaller around what feels safe. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings and changes have been present for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Most days, yes… I think so. I don’t know if it’s literally every minute of the day, but that low, sour feeling is usually there underneath things. Fear, guilt… even when nothing’s actually happening. I’m not sure if that counts as “most of the day,” but it does feel like it follows me around a lot.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings have been significantly interfering with your ability to work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, in some ways. I don’t want to overstate it, because I’m not working right now, so it’s not like I’m calling out of a job or anything. But I have missed Chamber application deadlines, and I keep putting my catering plans on hold.\n\nIt’s the paperwork, and following up with jobs, and dealing with people… I used to be able to handle that more reliably, and now I just kind of freeze or avoid it. At home I still do the basic things, but my world has gotten smaller, if that makes sense. I see friends less, too, because I stay closer to what feels safe. Is that the kind of interference you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of not caring and the low, sour feeling are getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say they’re getting worse, exactly. It’s more like… they’ve been hanging around, and some days feel heavier than others.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic if it isn’t. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear downhill slide, just that it hasn’t really lifted the way I keep hoping it will. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings have been present for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… definitely more than two weeks. I mean, I wish it was just a couple weeks.\n\nIt’s been going on since the robbery at that festival job… about two years and three months ago now.
